अनुसंधान परिषद ने निम्नलिखित संस्था को प्राय-कर नियम , 1962 के 
नियम 6 ( ii ) के साप पठित प्राय -कर अधिनियम 1961 की धारा 35 
की उपधारा ( 1 ) के खण्ड ( ii ) के प्रयोजनों के लिए चिकित्सा अनुसंधान 
के क्षेत्र में वैमानिक अनुसंधान संगठन के प्रवर्ग के अन्तर्गत निम्नलिखित 
शो पर अनुमोदित किया है, अर्थात : - - 
( i ) यह कि संस्था चिकित्सिा अनुसंधान के क्षेत्र में वैज्ञानिक 

मनुसंधान के लिए प्राप्त राशियों का हिसाब पृथक से रखोगी । 
( ii ) यह कि संस्था प्रत्येक वित्तीय वर्ष के लिए अपने वैज्ञानिक 

मनुसंधान संबंधी क्रियाकलापों को एक वार्षिक विवरणी 
परिषद् को प्रति वर्ष 15 मई तक ऐसे प्ररूपों में प्रस्तुत 
फरेगी जो इस प्रयोजन के लिए अधिकपित किए जाएं और 
उसे सूचित किए जाएं ।

3. निरीक्षण का आधार – इस्पात की तार की सरियों का 
निरीक्षण निम्नलिखित रीति से यह सुनिश्चित करने के लिए किया जाएगा 
कि निर्यात के लिए प्राशयित ऐसी इस्पात की तार की लड़ियां अधिनियम 
की धारा 6 के अधीन मान्य मानक विनिर्देशो के अनुरूप है , 


( 5 ) उपनियम ( 1 ) के अधीन प्रत्येक सूचना तथा ऊपर उपनियम 
( 2 ) के अधीन घोषणा, यदि कोई हो , विनिर्माता के परिसर से 
परेषण के भेजे जाने के कम से कम दस दिन पहले अभिकरण तथा 
परिषद के कार्यालय में पहुंच जानी चाहिए । 

( 6 ) उपनियम ( 1 ) के अधीन सूचना तथा उपनियम ( 2 ) के 
प्रधीन घोषणा, यदि कोई हो , के प्राप्त होने पर , अभिकरण : - - 
( क ) अपना यह समाधान कर लेने पर कि विनिर्माण की प्रक्रिया 

के दौरान , विनिर्माता के उपाबंध 2 में अधिकथित पर्याप्त 
पवालिटी नियंत्रण का प्रयोग किया है तथा इस प्रयोजन के 
लिए मान्य मानक विनिदेशों के अनुसार उत्पाद के विनिर्माण 
के संबंध में परिषद द्वारा जारी किए गए अनुदेशों , यवि , 
कोई हो , का पालन किया है , यह घोषणा करते हुए मात 
दिन के भीतर प्रमाणपत्र जारी करेगा कि इस्पात की सार 
की लड़ियों का पारेषण निर्यात योग्य है । जहाँ विनिर्माता 
निर्यातकर्ता नहीं है यहाँ परेषण का वास्तविक परीक्षण किया 
जाएगा तथा ऐसी जांच तथा निरीक्षण जो 
प्रावश्यक हो अभिकरण द्वारा यह सुनिश्चित करने के 
लिए किया जाएगा कि उपरोक्त शर्तों का पालन किया गया

4 . निरीक्षणको प्रक्रिया . -- ( 1 ) इस्पात के तार की लड़ियों के परेषण का 
निर्यात करने का इच्छुक नियतिकर्ता अभिकरण को सविदात्मक विनिर्देशों के पूर्ण 
विवरण सहित लिखित रूप से एक सूचना देगा ताकि वह नियम 3 के अनुसार 
निरीक्षण कर सके । 


( 2 ) उपाबंध 2 में प्रधिकथित उत्पादन के दौरान पर्याप्त क्वा 
लिटी नियंत्रणो का प्रयोग करते हुए विनिर्मित इस्पात के सार की लड़ियों 
का निर्यात करने के लिए निर्यातकर्ता उपनियम (i ) मे वणित सूचना 
के साथ इस प्राशय की एक घोषणा भी करेगा कि निर्यात के लिए 
प्राशयित इस्पात की तार की लड़ियों का परेषण उपाबंध 2 में यथा 
अधिकथित पर्याप्त क्वालिटी नियंत्रणो का प्रयोग करके बनाया गया है 
तथा परेषण इस प्रयोजन के लिए मान्य मानक विनिर्देशों के अनुरूप 


( ख ) ऐसी दशा में जहां निर्यातकर्ता ने उपनियम ( 2 ) के अधीन 

यह घोषणा नहीं की है कि उपायंघ 2 में अधिकथित पर्याप्त 

बालिटी नियंत्रणों का प्रयोग किया गया है, अपना यह 
समाधान कर लेने पर कि इस्पात की तार की लड़ें इस 
प्रयोजन के लिए मान्य विनिर्देशो के अनुरूप है , जैसा उपाबंध 
3 में दिया गया है उस के अनुसार किए गए निरीक्षण 
या परीक्षण के प्राधार पर या दोनो के आधार पर ऐसे 
निरीक्षण करने के सात दिनों के भीतर यह घोषणा करते हुए 
प्रमाण- पत्न जारी करेगा कि इस्पात की तार की सहें निर्यात 
योग्य है : 


( 3 ) ऐसी प्रत्येक सूचना वा घोषणा या दोनों को एक प्रति उसी 
समय परिषद के निम्नलिखित कार्यालयों में से किसी एक कार्यालय को . 
जो निरीक्षण के स्थान से निकटतम है, भेजी जाएगी , अर्थात् : ... 


परन्तु जहां अभिकरण का इस प्रकार समाधान नहीं होता वहां 
बह उक्त सात दिनों की अवधि के भीतर निर्यातकर्ता को यह घोषणा 
करते हुए प्रमाण-पत्र जारी करने से इंकार कर देगा कि इस्पात की 
तार की लड़ियों का परेषण निर्यात योग्य है तथा निर्यातकर्ता को ऐसे 
इंकार की सूचना उसके कारणों सहित देगा ।

उपाय 3

परेषणानुसार निरीक्षण : - - 

( 1 ) उम्पान के तार की नलियों का परेषण निरीक्षण तथा पुनरीक्षण उसे लागू मानक विनिर्देशों से इसकी अनुरूपता सुनिश्चित करने के लिए 
किया जायगा । 


( 2 ) में निरीक्षण के प्रयोजनो के लिए, मिदेभी फ्रेता तथा निर्यात -फार्ता के मध्य हुई सहमति के अनुमार, माविदिक विनिर्वेश निरीक्षण का प्राधार 


होगे । 


माविदिक विनिर्देश मे जो विशेषताप विनिर्दिष्ट रूप से नहीं दी गई है उन विशेषतामों के लिए निरीक्षण का प्राधार अन्तर्राष्ट्रीय संगठमो जैसे 
पाई एस प्रो , आई ई मो , ए पी पाई तथा इसी प्रकार की सगठनो द्वारा जारी किए गए भारतीय या अन्य राष्ट्रीय मानकों या विनिर्देशों में दी गई 
विशेषताएं होंगी । 

( 3 ) नमूने तथा अनुरूपता के मापदण्ड के संबंध मे साविधीक मिनिर्देशो में विशिष्ट विशेषतामों के न होने की दशा मे वे मिशेषसाए लाग होगी 
जो सारणी 1 तथा 2 मे दी गई है ।

ORDER 
S . O . 1213. - .- Whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that in exercise of the powers conferred by section 6 of 
the Export ( Quality Control and Inspection ) Act , 1963 (22 
of 1963 ) , it is necessary and oxpedient to subject Steel Wire 
Strands to quality control and inspection prior to their ex 
port, for the sound development of export trade of India ; 

And whereas the Central Government has formulated cer 
tain proposals specified below for the said purpose and has 
forwarded the gamo to the Export Iospection Council as 
required by sub- rule ( 2 ) of rule 4 of the Export ( Quality 
Control and Inspection ) Rules , 1964 ; 

Now, theretore, in pursuance of the said sub -rule the 
Central Government horoby publishes the sald proposals for 
information of the public likely to be affected thereby . 


PROPOSALS 
( 1 ) To notify that Steel Wire Strands shall be subject to 
quality control and inspection prior to cxport ; 

(2 ) To Apecify the type of quality control and inspection 
in accordance with the draft Sleel Wire Strands (Quality Con 
trol and Inspection ) Rules , 1978 set out in Annexure I to 
this Order, as the type of quality control and inspection 
which would be applied to such Steel Wire Strands prior 
to export ; 
(3 ) To recognise -~

national standard specifications of a foreign country ;

ANNEXURE MI 


[(Sce Rule 3 (b )] 


Consignment-wise Inspection : 


( 1) The consignment of Steel Wire Strands shall be subjected to inspection and testing to cnsure conformity of the 
same to the standard specification applicable to it . 


(2) For the purposes of such inspection , tho contractual specification as agreed upon between the foreign buyer and 
the exporter shall form the basis of inspection , 


For the characteristics not specifically stipulated in the contractual specification the same as given in the Indian or 
other National Standards or specifications issued by International Organizations like ISO , IEC , API and the like , shall form 
the basis of inspection . 


( 3) In the absence of specific stipulation in the contractual specifications as regards sampling and criteria for confor 
mity : thc sanie laid down in Tables I and II appended hereto shall become applicable .

नस्थ हो गया है ।

ORDER 


New Delhi, the 7th April , 1978 


S . O . 1214 . -- The State Trading Corporation of India Ltd ., 
New Delhi were granted an import Licence No. P / D / 1404360 
dated 9 -10- 1974 and Letter of Authority in favour of Ms. 

Hindustan Ferodo Ltd., Ghatkopar , Bombay - 400086 for the

पैट्रोलियम, रसायन और उर्वरक मंत्रालय 

( पेट्रोलियम विभाग ) 

नई दिल्ली, 31 मार्च, 1978 
का०मा० 1230 - भारत सरकार के अधिसूचना के द्वारा जैसा कि 
यही संलग्न अनुसूची में प्रदर्शित किया गया है और पेट्रोलियम पाइप 
माइन ( प्रयोक्ता के भूमि अधिग्रहण अधिकार ) अधिनियम , 1962 के 
खण्ड 6 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) के अन्तर्गत प्रकाशित किया गया है , गुजरात 
राज्य के कलोल सेल फोन में उक्त परिशिष्ट भूमि में वेषन स्थल सं० 
के -48 से सी० टी० एफ़० तक पट्रोलियम के लिए भूमि उपयोग के अधिकार 
प्राप्त किए गए हैं । 

तेख एवं प्राकृतिक गैस प्रामोग ने उपर्युक्त नियम के खण्ड के उप - खण्ड 
( 1 ) की धारा ( 1 ) में निर्दिष्ट कार्य विनांक 2 - 6- 1974 से समाप्त कर 
दिया गया है । 

प्रतः अब पट्रोलियम पाइप लाइन के नियम 4 ( प्रेयोक्ता के भूमि 
अधिग्रहण पधिकार ) नियम, 1963 के अन्तर्गत सम्बर प्राधिकारी एतद्

का० आ० 12 36.----भारत सरकार के अधिसूचना के द्वारा जैसा कि 
यहाँ संलग्न अनुसूची में प्रदर्शित किया गया है और पेट्रोलियम पाइप 
लाइन ( प्रयोक्ता के भूमि अधिग्रहण अधिकार ) अधिनियम , 1962 के 
खण्ड 6 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) के अन्तर्गत प्रकाशित किया गया है, गुजरात 
राज्य के प्रकलेपवर तेल क्षेत्र में उक्त परिशिष्ट भूमि में वेधम स्थल सं० 
एस एन के- 1 से जी जी एस- 1 तक पैट्रोलियम के लिए भूमि उपयोग के 
अधिकार प्राप्त किए गए हैं । 

तेल एवं प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग ने उपर्युक्त नियम के खण्ड 7 के 
उपखण्ड ( 1 ) की धारा ( 1 ) में निर्दिष्ट कार्य दिनांक 15- 6- 76 से 
समाप्त कर दिया गया है । 

प्रतः अब पैट्रोलियम पाइप लाइन के नियम 4 ( प्रयोक्ता के भूमि 
अधिग्रहण अधिकार ) नियम , 1963 के अन्तर्गत सक्षम प्राधिकारी एतद् 
द्वारा उक्त तिथि को कार्य समाप्ति की तिथि अधिसूचित करते हैं । 


का० मा01237 – भारत सरकार के अधिसूचना के द्वारा जैसा कि यहाँ 
संलग्न अनुसूची में प्रदर्शित किया गया है मौर पट्रोलियम पाइप लाइन

के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) के अन्तर्गत प्रकाशित किया गया है, गुजरात राज्य के 
कलोल तेल क्षेत्र में उक्त परिशिष्ट भूमि में वेधन स्थल सं० डी एस 
के -113 से के 124 तक पैट्रोलियम के लिए भूमि उपयोग के अधिकार प्राप्त 
किए गए हैं । 

तेल एवं प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग ने उपर्युक्त नियम के खण्ड 7 के

12 . From the perusal of Section 3 of the Industrial Emp 
loyment ( Standing ) Orders Act , 1946 (Act 20 of 1946 ) Y 
find that this Section provides for submission of Draft Stand 
ing Orders . Section 5 thereof provides for cer 
lification of Stunding Orders unl Secuon 6 for all ippcal to 
the Appellate Authority . According to Section 7 of the In 
dustrial Employment (Standing Ordeis ) Act, 1946 , the Stand 
ing Orders shall, unless an appeal is preferred under Section 
6 come to operation on the expiry of 30 days from the date 
on which authenticated copics thereof arc sent under Sub 
Section ( 3 ) of Section 5 , or where an appeal has prefeito 
on the expiry of 7 days from the date on which copies of 
tho order of the Appcllate Authority are sent under Sub 
Section ( 2 ) of Sec . 6 . In the instant case certainly Draft 
Staring Orders were submitted for certification and they 
were certified as well but an appeal under section 6 was 
preferred and the certified Standing Orders were modified 
and as such these Certified Stinding Orders as certified inder 
Section 6 by the Appellate Authority would be deemed 10 
have come into force on the expiry of 7 days from 12th Feb 
ruary , 1971 in accordance with provisions of Section 7 of lhe 
Aut. Once these finally certified orders have come into opera 
tion the applicability of Scction 12 - A would tone quashe 
or abrogated . Section 12 - A of the Act 20 of 1946 pro 
vides in sub section ( 1 ) thul rot -with -standing inything con 
tained in Section 3 to 12 , for the period commencing on the 
date on which this Act becomes applicable to an Industrial 
Establishment and ending with the date on which Standing 
Orders has finally ccrtified under this Act come into operation 
in Section 7 in that establishment, the picscribed model Stand 
ing Orders shall be deemed to be adopted in that cotublish 
ment and the provisions of Sec . 8 . şub section ( 2 ) of Section 
13 and Section 13 - A shall apply to such Model Standing 
Order as they apply to Standing Orders so certificd . The 
words the Standing Orders as finally certified under this Act 
come into Operation under sec . 7 are very material to appre 
ciate the purport of Section 12 - A . According to these words 
the applicability of Model Standing Orders is abrogated the 
moment the Standing Orders as finally certified under this 
Act come into operation under Section 7 . Such has been 
the position in the instant case . The draft Standing Orders 
stood finally certified under Act 20 of 1946 on 12th Feb 
ruary , 1971 and would be under Section 7 of the Act deemed 
to havo come into operation from 19th February , 1971 and 
thereafter there would not be any question of Model Stand 
ing Orders coming into operation denovo . Mere fact that 
Hon ble Punjab and Delhi High Courts have stayed the 
Operation of final Certifisd Orders would no revive the 
applicability of the Model Standing Ordeis iir thc establish 
ment under question . Though an unfortunate situation arises 
but therc is no escape from the conclusion and it cannot 
be assumed that the Certified Standing Orders do not exist 
for the purpose of Section 7 or Section 12 - A in this esta 
blishment after 19th February , 1971 so as to bring into ope 
ration the Model Standing Ordere again . In this situation 
either the provisions of finally coitified Standing Orders would 
govern or the provisions of specific contract as given in para 
2 of letter of appointment copy Ex W . 1 would prevail. 
Whether under the finally certified Standing Oiders or under 
the provisions of Para 2 of letter of appointment, the proba 
tion can certainly be extend upto six months and in the instant 
caso original probation provided in the letter of appoint 
mont was for three months and Company had been given 
the right to extend the said probation and it was in fact ex 
tended by another three months and the services were 
terminated before thc copiiy of six months of probation 
period . Considering this position it would follow thit the 
Management was well within its right to extend the probation 
period of ibe workmati in question to a maximum of six 
monthy and it was open to the M2111gement to teimina c the 
services during the extended period of probation as well and 
this is what has happened in the instant casc , That being the 
position it would be difficult to Assume that the Manage 
ment was not well within its right to dispense with the scr 
vices or present workman Shri Ishwar Singh and accordingly 
it cannot be said that the termination of civice of the workman 
in the instant case is not justified and I hold accordingly .

AWARD 
The Central Govt. vide its order No. L -29012 / 21 /74 - LR . 
IV dated the 30th November, 1974 made the following re 
ference to Industrial Tribunal at Faridabad : 
Whether tho action of the Manageinent of M / s . Dalnija 

Dadri Cement Liipited , Charkhi Dadri in terminat 
ing the services of Shri Man Singh , Clerk . w . e . f. 
the 11th January , 1974 is justified ? If not, to what 

relief is the workman entitled ? 
2 . On receipt of the said reference requisite notices were 
Kent to the respective parties and a statement of claim was 
filed by the workman in which it has been submitted that 
the workman had been in fact working in the Quarrles of 
M / s . Dalmia Dadri Cement Limited , Charkhi Dadri since 
20 - 1 - 1966 as clerk reguariy ; that after settlement dated 
9 - 7 - 1973 the petitioner was issued an appointment letter now 
copy Ex , W - 1 ; that the Company extended the probation 
period mentioned in Ex . W - 1 by another three months ag 
given in Ex, W - 2 ; that the Company ultimately terminated 
the services of tho workman w . e . f. 12 - 1 - 1974 vide letter copy 
Ex . W - 3 ; that the said termination is illegal and unjustificd 
and malafide in as much as the Management have no right 
to extend the period of probution and there were no grounds 
for terminating the services of the workman . There are other 
allegations in the statement of claim which are not relevant 
to the order of reference . 

3 . Thereafter the Management filed a written statement 
contcnding inter - alia that the allegations not relevant to and 
beyond the scope of order of reference and are uncalled for; 
that no demand notice was served upon the Management by 
the workman or the Union ; that the workman had been cm 
ployed as stated in Ex . W - 1 ; that the period of probation had 
been extended in accordance with law and Standing Orders 
applicable to the respondent; that the order of termination is 
valid and not liable to be vet aside. 

4 . Upon these pleadings of the parties the following 
issues were framed by Shri O . P . Sharma on 3 - 4 - 1975 ; 


In order to prove this issue the work man has produced 
Ex , W - 4 which is true copy of the demand notice which served 
upon the Management. He has also filed Ex W - 5 , copy of 
the letter received by the workman from the Management 
From the perusal of these two documents it is established 
beyond any shadow of doubt that a demand and dispute had 
been raised by the workman and repudiated by the Madage 
ment and therefore this issue is decided in favour of the work 
man and against the Management . 


9 . Issuc No. 3 ; 

Ex . W - 1 is the copy of Letter of appointment filed by the 
workınan und from the perusal thereof ( find that the work 
man have been appointed in pusuance of his application 
dated 10 - 7 - 1973 w . e . f . 14 - 7 - 1973 as a Loading Made and from 
the perusal of para No. 2 thereof I find that it has been 
provided theicin us follows : 
" That you will be on probation for three months and 

the Company has a right to extend your probationi 
period or dispense with your services at any time 
without notice or compensation in lieu of nolice 
without assigning any reason to you cven before 

completion of your probation period . 
It has been submitted by the Id . counsel for the Management 
that the Management had extended the period of probation in 
accordance with para 2 of the appointment letter and the 
said extension was valid and consequently termination was effec 
ted within this extended period of probation 
Ex. W - 2 is the letter dated 12 - 10 - 1973 whereby the period 
of probation was extended by another three months upto 
13 - 1 - 1974 . Letter dated 11- 1 - 1974 , copy Ex . W - 3 , shows that 
the service had been terminated w . c . f . 12 - 1 - 1974 . Thus on facts 
it is established that the services of the workman were termi 
nated within the extended period of probation , 


Issues : 
1 . Whether the present refercncc is bad in law for 

reasons given in the preliminary objection A of 

the Written Statement ? (On Management) . 
2 . Whether the demand the subject matter of the pre 

sent reference was first l aised on the management 
and rejected by it before taking up the matter for 
conciliation ? If not, with what effect ? (On work 
man ) .

SCHEDULE II 
“ Whether the demands of the workmen employed in the 

Iron Ore Mines of the management whose names 
are specfied in Schedule I for implementation of the 
recommendations of the Central Wage Board for the 
Iron Ore Mining Industry are justified if so , to what 

relief are they untitled and from what date ? " 
2 . M / s . Mining and Transporting Company, Party No. 14 , 
has asserted in an application dated 16 - 9 - 1976 that it is not 
operating any iron ore mine and the name of the Company 
has been erroneously brought in the reference . They had 
made a similar prayer even in March 1968 . This party is not 
governed by the stay order of the Calcutta High Court , Jospite 
of the notices and no union has come forward to iebut this 
fact. The reference is squately relating to the implementation 
of the recommendations of Central Wage Board for the iron 
Ore mining industries. Hence the Company which is not 
operating any iron ore mine industry can hardly be a party 
to such a reference . Under the circumstances , no disputo 
award as prayed for is given with respect to this Company . 
The name of the said Company be struck of from the Schedule 
I of the reforence .

Short Recital of the Cases :

rences between employer and its workmen in respect of or 
arising out of the first and second interim reports and the 
final report of the Central Wage Board for Iron Ore Mining 
Industry set up by the Government of India or in connection

2 . The following persons /workmen will not be covered

3 . Minimum Toal Emoluiments 

The total minimum emoluments of the lowest paid unskilled 
workers corresponding to the All India Average consumer 
price index number 166 ( 1949 - 100 ) with effect from 
1 - 1 - 1967 include basic wages , Deamess Allowance , cash value 
of food grains concession and two interim wage increases as 
recommended by the Wage Board for Iron Ore Mining 
Industry . 

4 . Classification 

Tho Iron Ore Mining of the Company shall be grouped 
" T " Mines as per categorisation recommended by the Wigo 
Board for Iron Oro Mining Industry .

6 . Dearness Allowance 

The Doarness Allowance should consist of Two parts, 
one of which should vary with variation in the cost of living . 
The dearness allowance which is a part of the total emoluments 
corresponding to the average All India Consumer Price Index 
number 166 (1949 — 100 ) will not be reduced unless Index 
number going below 144 . The other part of the dearness 
allowance should rise or fall depending upon the changes in 
the index number abovo 166 . The following will be the monthly 
or daily rates of Dearness Allowance .

10 . Permanency of Workers . 

All persons with six months continuous service should be 
given facilities which are normally available to permanent 
workers. 

11. Holiday and Leave Facilities 

Existing procedure will be followed i.e . 10 ( ten ) paid holl 
days which should include Republic Day and Independenco 
Day and (b ) 18 days sick leave with full pay .

6 . DEARNESS ALLOWANCE : 

The Dearnoss Allowance should consist of Two Parts , onc 
of which should vary with variation in the cost of living . The 
dcarness allowance which is a part of the total emoluments 
corresponding to the average All India Consumer Price Index 
Number 166 ( 1949 -100 ) will not be reduced unless Index Num 
ber goes below 144 . The other part of the dearness allowance 
should rise or fall depending upon the changes in the index 
number above 166 . The following will be monthly or daily

7. VARIABLE DEARNESS ALLOWANCE : 

( a ) For every variation of 2 points in the All India Consumer 
Price Index number 166 ( 1949 - 100 ) the dearness allowance 
should vary at Rs, 1 . 50 per month for monthly rated workers 
and at 0 . 06 paise per day for daily rated workers . There 
shall be no adjustment for rise and fall below 2 points, 


( c ) With cffcct liom 1- 1 -69 till 31- 3 -69 a piecc rated 
raising workman will be entitled to get efter deduction of 
cash value of foodgrains alrcudy supplied , the net rate of 
Rs. 5 , 21 per 36 Cft , or its cquivalent in Cum . at Roida Iron 
Mines . These rates are inclusive of basic wave , dcurless 
allowance and variable dearness allowance and phased in 
crements . 


( d ) With ellect from 1 - 4 -69 till 30 - 6 - 69 a piccc lated 
raising workinan will be entitled to get efter deduction of 
cash value of foodgrains alrcady supplied , the net raise of 
Rs . 5 , 03 per 36 cft . Or its equivalent in Cum . at Roida 
Iron Mines . These rate are inclusive of basic wage , dcar 
nçse allowance and variahle dcarness allowance and phased 
increments . 


( c ) With cffect from 1-6 - 1969 , the directly employed 
piece rated workman will get supply foodgrains cqual to the 
quantity issucd and at the concessional price charged at pre 
sent to such piece rated workmen employed by the contractors 
in Roidu lion Mines of the Management . 


(b ) In view of the practical difficulty in calculating the arreur 
wages and the amount entitled to on account of variablo Dear 
ness Allowance, it is mutually agreed that for the year 1967 
the All India Consumors Price Index Number is taken as 204 
and the year 1968 at 212 , and till 31 - 3 -69. From 1 -4 -69 till 
30 -6 -69 the All India Index number is taken as 206 . Subse 
qucnt to 1- 7-69 , there will be a revision of variable Dearness 
Allowance for every quarter basing the Index number of the 
previous quarter as the standard . The Dearness Allowance 
should be treated as part of the totul emoluments of the cmp 
loyees , 


From 1 - 7 - 1969 the rates will bc calculated every quarter 
basing on the variable Deurness Allowance applicable for 
every quarter basing the Index Number of the previous quar 
ter as a standard applicable for arriving at the variable 
Dearness Allowance .

and the case was fired for determination of iemaining issues.

do not proposo to Icad any oral evidence . The documents

arguments . Before arguments were heard the parties appeared 
and filed a compromise and after going through the compro 
mise and acttleinent I have come to the conclusion that the 
compromise and settlement I have come to the conclusion

by the Industrial Tribunal, Faridabad the case was trans 
ferred to this Tribunal and after it was registered notices 
were issued to the respective partics and the case was fixed 
for evidence of the parties . The parties did not lead any 
oral evidence rather Shri Bhim Sain and Shri S . N . 
Bhandari, representatives of respective workman and the 
Management stated that parties do not propose to lead 
any oral evidence . The documents placed on record by 
respective particy be exhibited and read into evidence and 
the case bc fixed for arguments . In consequence these 
documents were exhibited as Ex , W - 1 to Ex . W - 10 and 
M - 1 to Ex . M - 5 and he case was adjourned for arguments. 
Before arguments were heard the parties appeared and 
filed a compromise and after going through the compro 
mise and settlement I have come to the conclusion that 
the compromisc is in the interest of the workman and 
therefore it was ordered to be recorded . Accordingly state 
ment of workman Shri Kamal Singh , his representative 
Shri Bhim Sain and the representative of the Management 
Shri O , P . Gupta were recorded vide my order dated 9th 
February , 1978 . They have stated that the parties have 
arrived at a settlement. The settlement is Ex . S / 1 . It bears 
our signatures . The settlement has been signed on behalf 
of the D . D . C . by Shri S . C . Vasishta . An award may be 
made in terms of the settlement Ex , S / l . This settlement 
bears the signatures of Shri Bhim Sain and Shri O . P . 
Gupta as well. Accordingly an award in terms of settle 
ment Ex, S / 1 is made. The parties shall be bound by the 
settlement Ex . S / 1 which shall for part of this award . 
The terms of settlement would thus deemed to be incor 
porated in this award and the award in terms of settle 
ment Ex . S / 1 is mado .

gava J, observed that : - - - 


Following these guiding principles laid down by the Supremo 
Court it is clear in the facts and circumstances proved in 
the present case that there is Arcal disputo , and , as the 
dispute has been sponsored by the Union of workmen , the 
dispute is between the management and the workman . Thus 
the first two ingradients of Sec . 2 ( k ) exist in the prçsent 
set of circumstances. The third ingrcdicnt which requires 
community of interest of the workmen with person whoso 
cause is espoused , also stands satisfied for the following 
rçuşons . 


" If the man is employed because he possesses such 

faculties and they enable him to produce something 
as a creation of his own , he will have to be held 
to be employed on technical work , even though in 
carrying out that work , he may have to go through 
a lot of manual labour. If on the other hand , he 
is merely employed in supervising work of others 
the fact that, for the purpose of proper supervision , 
he is required to have technical knowledyc will not 
convcrt his supervisory work into technical work . 
The work of giving advice and guidance cannot 
he held to be an employment to do technical 
A person with technical qualification can on that 
account be employed in a supervisory capacity and 
in such a casc he will be held to be employed to 
do supervisory work so that in order to be a work 
man he must not be excmpted under exceptions ( 4 ) . " 


14 . Shri Kamla Pandey is the Sccretary of the Union vide 
mention of this fact in the failure report Ex, W / 1 . The 
same Union of which he is the Secretary hay espoused this 
dispute on behalf of tho workmen of the Colliery . The 
workmen for whose benefits the Secretary raised the demand 
and led a demonstration are directly and intrinsically inter 
csted in seeing that the Secretary is not victimised or punished 
more severly than what is Warranted by the excess , if any , 
committed by him while so leading the demonstration und 
espousing the cause of the workmen , otherwise if the secre 
tary of the Union is Icft free to be molested by the manage 
ment on account of his trade union activities and for fi 
for their rights the poor workmen will have nonc - else to 
fall back upon in tinies of nceds because in that case in 
future everyone would be apprehensive to lead any such 
demonstration for projecting their demands. There is thus 
a vital link and community of interest between the work 
men represented by the Union and their Sccrctary who has 
been punished , even though that sçcretary is not a workman . 
Thus the third condition for bringing the dispute within the 
Jelinition of Sec . 2 ( k ) stands fulfilled and thus the dispute 
so raised certainly becomes an industrial dispute . It is , there 
fore , held that the objection against the validity of the refer 
cnce on account of the non -existence of an industrial dispute 
has no forco .

In the present cuse Shri Kamla Pandey , Mining Sirdar , is 
not using his technical knowledge for creating something 
by his manual labour and by using his technical knowledge . 
His technical knowledge is utilised simply for proper super 
vision that safety conditions are maintained by the workers . 
Therefore as per principles laid down by the Supreme Court 
in the aforesaid case Mining Sirdars cannot but be categori 
sed into supervisory staff _ falling under exceptions ( 4 ) of 
Scc . 2 ( s ) of the Industrial Disputes Act. 

12 . Even though as held above , Shri Kamla Pandey is not 
a workman being in the supervisory capacity and drawing 
more than Rs. 500 / - as wages , the reference remains valid 
becausc on behalf of the workmen the Union was conpetent 
to raise a dispute about any person who was not work 
man . In the case of workmen Dimakuchi Tea Estatc 
Vs . Management ( 3 SCLJ - 1898 SC ) the Supreme Court , while 
interpreting the broadly worded expression any person used 
in Section 2 ( k ) which defines an industrial dispute , carved out 
two crucial liinitation circumscribing the ambit of that ex 
pression and the significant of those two limitations SO 
carved out by the Supreme Court is that the person regarding 
whom that dispute is raised must be one in whose employ 
ment or non -employment etc , the workmen raising the dis 
pute are directly or substantially interested . 


15 . Even then it remains to be seen whether the findings 
given by the Enguiry Officer were perverse , Perversity in 
the findings can be cither because it is based on no evidence , 
or because no reasonable mai could have reached that con 
clusion on the basis of the evidence on record . It was so 
held in Central Bank of India Ltd . Vs. Prakash Chand Jain 

969 - 11 -LLJ 377 ( 380 ) . It cannot be tormed as perverse 
simply because a different view could be possible . 

16 . As regards this aspect 1 an of the view after close 
scrutiny of tho evidence recorded by the Enquiry Officer 
that his appraisal of tho evidence was not such as can be 
said to be perverse in the sense that no reasonable man 
could have comc to that finding Nowhere the cvidence 
against Amal Mukherjee was read against Kamla Pandey 
and no prejudice appcars to have been caused because of 
the joint enquiry when both the delinquents were chargcc 
with respect to the same incident and identical question of 
law and facts were involved . I am , therefore, of the vicw 
that the findings were not perverse . 


13 . In Standard Vacuum Refining Conipany Vs . Their 
Workmen ( 3 SCL 2201) the Supreme Court further analysed 
the definition of industrial dispute given in Sec . 2 ( k ) and 
said that it requires threc things viz ., 

" (i) there should be a dispute or difference ; 
( ii ) the dispute or difference should be between emp 

loyer and worknicn etc . ; and 
( iii ) the dispute or difference inust be connected with 

the employment or non -employment of any person . 
The first part thus refors to the facturn of real and substan 
tial dispute , the second part refers to the parties to the dis 
pute and the third to the subject matter of the dispute , " jf 
the first two ingredients are present then the Supreme Court 
said on page 2205 in the aforesaid case that : 


17. So fiir as the quantum of punishment is concerned 
I am of the view that there is no cause for interference . 
A person acting in supervisory capacity who holds the res 
ponsible charge of looking after the sufety of mines is ex 
pected to behave fiz a proper manncr even while pressing 
the demands of the workinen before the ofliccrs . Thumping 
of the table and keeping the A . L . W . O . under sherao so that 
he is unable to even take water or persuc the normal routine 
of life was nothing but a serious matter . I am , therefore , 
not inclined to interfere with the punishment awarded by 
the punishing authority. Reference is answered accordingly .

5 . The case of Shri Hiralal , workman , is that the domestic 
enquiry was not proper because :- - - 

(1) the Enquiry Officer was biased ; 
(ii) the delinquent was not allowed to cross -cxamine 

some of the witnesses ; 
(iii ) the witnesses were influenced by the management 

and were threatened to depose against the delin 

quent ; 
(iv) no opportunity to produce defence evidence was 

glven ; 
( v ) copy of the report of Enquiry Officer was not sup 

plied : 
( vi) second show cause noticc was not given , nor any 

hearing was granted at that slage ; 
( vii) the report did not discuss the evidence ; 
(viii) the finding was perverse ; and 
( ix ) the factum of acquittal by the criminal Court was 

not con uidered by the management even after the 
dismissal. 


For all these reasons it is alleged that the dismissal way bad 
and the delinquent deserves to be reinstated with back wagce . 
The successor Company is alleged to be liable to reinstate 
The workman . 


6 . The case of the crstwhile employer M / & Newton Chi 
ckli Colliery is that long before the reference the coal mines 
was nationalised and the private company had been deprived 
of all its assets , hence it was improperly impleaded . 

7. The case of the successor Government Company is that 
the enquiry was proper and vaild and the order of dismissal 
15 unassailable . In any case the Government Company has 
been exonerated of all financial liability accruing against 
the erstwhile ownes and is thuy neither liable to pay back 
wages nor under an obligation to reinstate the workman even 
if it is held that the dismissal wos not valid .

Dated , 28th March , 1978 , 

AWARD 
This is a reference made by the Government of India 
in the Ministry of Labout vide it s order No. L - 22012 / 9 / 73 
LRII dated 18th February , 1975 , for the adjudication of the 
following industrial dispute by this Tribunal : 
" Whether the action of the management of Messrs New 

ton Chickli Colliery limited , Post Box No. 45, Post 
Office Tharja , District Dhanbad (now under the 
management of Coal Mines Authority , Newton 
Chickli Colliery , Post Office Parasia , District Chhind 
wara , (Madhya Pradesh ) is justified in terminating 
the services of Shri Hiralal , Electrical Helper ? If 
not, lo what relief is he entitled and from what 

date ? " . 
2 . It is not disputed that on 3 - 5 - 1967 the delinquent 
workman , Shri Hiralal, was served with a charge -sheet that 
hc had disobcyed the direction of Shri Parás Nath Mitra , 
Electric Fitter , to dismantle pump and secondly he had 
committed theft of about 150 ft, cable . Domestic enquiry 
was conducted by Shri K . Ç . Sharma, Office Superintendent , 
of the then Company . During the course of enquiry Babu 
Lal, Panna Singh , Faqira , Bhag Chand , Shehadhar Chakra 
vorty and Paras Nath Mila wcrc cxamined . Of these Shri 
Paras Nath Mitra and Shri Panna Singh were actually cross 
examined by the delinquent. The statements of the rest of 
the witnessess do not indicate that they were cross - examined 
by him . The Enquiry Officer submitted his report , copy 
of which was not supplied to Shri Hiralal. Second show 
calişe notice proposing the punishment was not given by the 
disciplinary Authority which simply passed an order No . 
2026 / 128 dated 31st May, 1967 dismisging Shri Hiralal from 
service on the ground that both the charges were proved 
against him , 

3 . Shri Hiralal was simultaneously facing prosecution 
before a criminal Court for the said incident of theft of 
cable . The Enquiry Oficer did not wait for the result of 
the criminal case but proceeded to complete thc cnquiry . 
Long after the awarding of the aforesaid punishment of dis 
missal by the disciplinary authority , the criminal Court passed 
a judgment of acquittal in favour of Shri Hiralal on 11 - 12 - 1968 
holding that he did not committed any such theft for which 
he was charged . All this happened when M / s . Newton 
Chickli Colliery Company was the owner and manager of the 
colliery . 

4 . Industrial dispute against the validity of dismissal was 
raised and when the conciliation failed the present reference 
was made to this Tribunal in the year 1975 as said above . 
Meanwhile with effect from 31st January , 1973 the managc 
ment of the Colliery was taken over by the Government 
of India Company i. e . the Coal Mines Authority and 
after a few months the ownership of the Colliery vested in 
that Government Company. The latter development is that 
Coal Mines Authority has now been replaced by the Western 
Coulfields Limited which is a subsidiary Company of Coal 
India Limited , M / s . Newton Chickli Colliery thus lost 
ownership and control over the Colliery in which Shri Hiralal 
wag once employed after the nationalisation of coalficids , 


8. There is no evidence that the Enquiry Officer was 
biased or was influenced in any manner. The statement of 
tho Enquiry Officer on this point stands unrebutted . 

9 . When the domestic enquiry opened with thc cxamina 
tion of the delinqucnt himself , Shri Hiralal, said that the 
witnesses proposed to be examined by the management shall 
be his witnesses as well. This statement indicated that he 
was not intending to produce or examine any o her witnesses 
in defence . Even the statcmcnta of the management witnesses 
examined during the course of enquiry to show that no 
other person was present at the place where the theft was 
alleged to be committed , hence there was no question of 
cxamining any other person in defence , The Enquiry Officer 
has said that full opportunity to produce the defence was 
given to Shri Hiralal. There is no rebuttal of this evidence . 
It is held that the objection on this count has no force . 


10 . The Enquiry Officer stated ar M . W . 1 that from 
the aforesaid statement of the delinquent that the witnesses 
cxamined by the management shall be his witnessey as well , 
he inferred that the delinquent did not want to cross - examino 
any witness . Yet he allowed him to cross - examine the wit 
nekes whenever he wanted to avail of that opportunity . 
Accoiding to him the delinquent did not cross - examine other 
than thc two witnesses mentioned above. There is no rebuttal 
on this point and simple omission to write at the end of 
thove statements that the clelinquent refused to cross - examina 
them was not of much consequence . In fact those other 
witnesses were not the eye witnesses and the hear say evidence 
which they had given did not require any real cross-examina 
tion . The objection on this count has also no force . 

11. It was not necessary to give a copy of the report 
of the Enquiry Officer to the delinquent no way it neces 
sary to give a second notice to the delinquent for howing 
cause against the proposed punishment. It was so held in 
Shahadra ( Delhi Sharanpur light Railways Company Limited 
Vs. Union of India ( 1969- 1-ILJ 734 (744 ) S. C .). Thus 
again these objections carry little wait , 

12 . The delinquent was facing two charges, one of dis 
obedience and the other of the commission of theft so far ay

The report of the Enquiry Officer is, therefore , scrupulously 
silent about it . In support of that the order passed by the 
disciplinary authority on the back side of the report of the 
Enquiry Officer and the typed order of dismissal passed by 
him on 31- 5 - 1967 A9 Ex . M / 3 , Say in clçar words with 
reference to the charge sheet dated 3 - 5 - 1967 that both the 
charges were fully proved . Besides being mechanical such 
an order wag nothing but a perverse order . Tho disciplinary 
authority came to the conclusion that the two misconducts 
proved constituted such 2 serious matter that the extreme 
panalty of dismissal was necessary in the case i. e . the dismissal 
was the result of the combined effect of thc crroneous belicf 
of disciplinary authority that both the charges were proved . 
Thc punishmont so awarded on the basis of such an erronçous 
belief and mechanical exercise of jurisdiction cannot be up 
held . For all the l casons discussed above the punishment 
90 awarded stands vitiated . 


domestic enquiry was concerned , while the criminal pro 
secution was confined to the incident of theft only . Thus the 
criminal prosecution did not cover the whole ground of the 
domestic enquiry . The Enquiry Oficer was , hercfore , not 
unjustified in not stuying the çnquiry , nor for that reason 
the enquiry can be said to be vitiated as was observed in 
Tata Oil Mills Co . Ltd . Vs. Workmen (AIR 1965 SC 155 ) . 

13 . However, that is not the end of the matter. The 
report of the Enquiry Officer only cnumerates the namcs of 
the witncsses examined and gays rather presumptuously that 
Shri Hiralal is at fault 49 his intention was motivated with 
pilfering of cable by cutting into pieces . No witness had 
seen him cutting the same. Crime or even misconduct of 
theft is not committed merely by entertaining intentions or 
motives to commit the same. The report proceeds further 
to say that , he (Hiralal) and in his possession lack Saw and 
frame with blade cutting plier and screw driver , which goes 
to prove his intention of abctting or attempting of pilferring 
of cable . Here again th¢ Enquiry Officer sccms to have 
drawn presumptions from the fact that the delinquent was 
in possession of certain tools , without adverting to the ex 
planation which he had given on this point. Moreover the 
inference drawn is that the delinqucnt was either abetting the 
commission of theft by someone- elve or was attempting 10 
do it himself , i. c . the Enquiry Officer was not sure as to 
who was the real thief. Such a dubious and indecisive report 
could not be the basis for holding that the definite charge of 
commission of theft was proved against him , when the Enquiry 
Officer himself, a9 said above , was not sure in his report as 
to whether the delinquent had himself committed or attempted 
the theft or he was aiding or abetting someoneelse . The 
disciplinary authority thus acted mechanically and did not 
apply its mind . Without adverting to the evidence on record 
he passed the order holding that the guilt of theft was proved 
against Shri Hiralal , 


17 . As discussed above , though the procedure which was 
followed by the Enquiry Officer was proper and though full 
opportunity to defend was given , yet the order of punishment 
was vitiated because the findings were perverse and the 
disciplinary authority mechanically punished the delinquent 
on a charic which was not proved at all . Firstly where the 
proper opportunity to defend is granted in the enquiry but 
simply the punishment is vitiated , there is no necd to re 
open the enquiry for affording a fresh opportunity to the 
management to prove the facts and secondly such reopening 
of cnquiry is now not necessary because competent criminal 
Court has held Shri Hiralal not guilty of the charge of theft, 
not because of any technical flaw or on account of giving 
benefit of doubt, but on merits after wholly disbelieving the 
testimony of the star witness , Shri Paray Nath 
Mitra . I Am , therefore , of the view that there 
is no case for reopening the enquiry and giving 
an opportunity to the management before this Tribunal 
to produce any evidence . The order of dismissal is void 
and nonest and under the circumstances Shri Hiialal 
should be deemed to have continucd in service as before . 
M / s. Newton Chickli Collicry Company , which was rightly 
impleaded as a party in the case is therefore bound to pay 
the emoluments of Shri Hiralal from the date of dismissal 
to the date on which the mine wag nationalised . 


14 . Neither the Enquiry Officer gave any reason in the 
report as to how and why he believed the witnesses of the 
management in preference to the statement given by the 
celinquent nor the disciplinary authority gave any reason 
for coming to the conclusion of the guilt. Merely saying 
in the report of the Enquiry Officer that the statcment of the 
delinquent was exaggerated was , besides being insuflicient, 
quite incongruous . One could say that the explanation so 
given by him was not satisfactory about his presence near the 
ypot or about tho possession of those tool but the word cxag 
geration does not carry that sense . There seems to be no 
exaggeration in the statement of the delinquent. Thus what 
cver poor reason is given by the Enquiry Officer in the report 
is nothing but false and absured . It has been held in Cooper 
Engineering Limited Vs. P . P . Munda ( 1975 ( 12 ) SCLJ 354 
( 356 ) that the Enquiry Officer should give reasons in support 
of his conclusion in the report but if he had not done so the 
disciplinary authority should give reasons adverting to the 
evidence on record . 

15 . No witness , not even Shri Paraş Nath Mitra , had seen 
Shri Hiralal cutting the cable . Shri Hiralal s immediate denial 
was not considered by the Enquiry Officer . As said above his 
explanation was also not considered . Shri Parag Nath Mitra 
stated that he had heard the sound of cable cutting and 
thercafter he saw Shri Hiralal coming from that side in a 
state of nervourness . This is the only evidence from which 
presumption of commission of thcft has been raised . All 
other witnesses have stated only what they heard from Shri 
Paras Nath Mitra . Where is the guarantce that Shri P . N . 
Mitra was telling truth on these two points . The first charge 
of diobediance related to the dcfiance of the orders issued 
by this very witness . No evidence was produced on that 
charge . Even Shri Mitra did not say a word about it . It is 
thus clear that the first chargc way falsely laid against Shri 
Hiralal by this witness , Shri P . N . Mitra . It appears that 
when he could not substantiate that charge he tried to impli 
cato Shri Hiralal in the second charge of theft by fabricating 
the story of hearing the sound of cable cutting etc . Morc 
over this witness , Shri P . N . Mitra , has been wholly dis 
believed by the learnej Magistrate in the criminal case . The 
Enquiry Officer has not give any reason 49 to how he 
believed the statement of Shri Mitra as a gospel truth and 
this mechanical approach makes his findings as perver cas 
no reasonable man could have come to such a conclusion on 
the basis of guch shattered and flimsy picce of evidence which 
was wholly unworthy of credence . 


18 . This brings us to the question of liability of the 
Western Coalfields Limited to reinstate the workman , Shri 
Hiralal, and pay him back wages from the date of nationa . 
lisation onwards. This Government Company has sought 
shelter and complete exoneration under the provisions of 
Coal Mines Nationalisation Act, 1973 . Firstly , nationalisation , 
according to the policy of the State as declared in the pream 
ble to Act 26 of 1973 aims to provide for acquisition for 
transfer of thọ right, title and interest of the owners in res 
pect of coal mincs.. . .. .... in order that the ownership and 
control of such resources arc vested in the Stato and thereby 
so distributed as best to subserve the common good . This 

eology had been adopted from Clause ( b ) of Article 39 
of the Constitution which says that the State shall in parti 
cular dircct its policy towards sccuring that the ownership 
and control of the material resources of the community are 
50 distributed as best to subserve the common good . The 
words common good as used in that Articlc as well as in 
the Preamble of Act No. 26 of 1973 are wide enough to 
include the interest of the workmen as well . House Nationa 
lisation of ownership of the coal miney should subscrve the 
interest of the victimised workmen rather than leave these 
poor citizens of the country on the threshold of hunget 
and starvation arising out of such unemployment. No pro 
vision of the Coal Mines Nationalisation Act should thercforo 
ne so interpreted as to put the security of service of a victi 
mised labourer to joopardy specially when Section 14 speci 
fically provides that every person who is a workman of the 
crstwhile Company shall continue to remain workman under 
the Government Company and thereby confirms this aspect 
of security of service to all the labourers, by saying that 
the Act is meant to operate against the owners and not 
against the labourers, 


19 . Moreover interpretation of the Industrial Laws and in 
a way this Nationalisation Act can also be classified as an 
Industrial law , should recognise the socially vital factor of 
industrial jurisprudence and constitutional mandate of Arti 
clo 42 which directs the State to secure all workers just 
and human conditions of work . Security of employment 
is the first requisite of a worker s lifc as observed by the 
Supremo Court in L . Michael and anotber Vs. Johnson 


16 . The enquiry papers show that the management exa 
mined no witness for proving the charge of disobedience .

4 . Fx , W / 3 is an application moved by Shri Manşingh 
on 9 - 6 - 1975 . It was addressed to Vice President of the 
Union at Chandametta . In this application Shri Mansingh 
specifically stated that Shri Wasik , Contractor, had refused 
him work because he could not attend on 25 - 1 - 1975 on 
account of illness . This admission is sufficient to show that 
he was an employee of the Contractor. This portion of the 
application was specifically put to Shri Mansigh ( W . W . 1 ) in 
cross examination and he had no explanation to offer. 


take him back in employment. This again goes to confirm 
that Shri Mansingh was not in employment after 25 - 1 - 1975 . 
It is thus obvious that by mistake a stated by Shri 
Sachdeva , Manager, on oath , he passed an order of transfer 
of this labour from Newton Chickli B Colliery to New 
Chickli A Colliery sometime on or about 23rd Febriigi y , 
1975. The Manager of Newton Chickli Collery did not accept 
him on transfer, hence he had to return back . Meanwhile 
Shri Sachdeva had come to know that he was not an employee 
of the Company and as such the whole matter was dropped . 
Transfer order was an obvious mistake hecause admittedly 
Shri Mansingh was not in employment after 25 - 2 - 75. Such 
mistaken order could not confer any status of a workman upon 
Shri Mansingh . 


5 . The Management examined Shri Gulam Hussain ( M . W . 1 ) 
who was the Munshi of the Contractor . He has stated in 
clear terms that Shri Mansingh was an employee of tho 
Contractor . His emoluments were paid by the Contractor 
after encashing his own bills advanced against the contract . 
He had proved Wage -sheets ( Ex . M / ? and Ex , M / 3 ) and 
further proved copy of the Form A register ( Ex , M / 4 ) . 
These documents were maintained by the Contractor and 
had to be countersigned by an officer of the Company in 
order to ensure that the payment was made to the workman 
in his presence . They go to show that Shri Mansingh was 
an employce of the Contractor. The licence of Shri Wasik 
for working as a Contractor given under the Contract Labour 
(Regulation and Abolition ) Act, 1970 was with Shri Gulam 
Hussain but the learned Counsel for the Union did not allow 
him to produce the game. 


9 When the contract labour was abolishol in October. 
1975, in this Colliery those who were working with the 
Contractor at that time were duly screened and after medical 
check up were recruited on the regular rolls of the Company. 
This advantage was given to only those labourers who were 
on the rolls of the Contractor on the date of the abolition 
of the contract system . Shri Mansingh was not on the rolls 
of the Contractor on that date hence hills case could not be 
considered . This method of screening and regularisation also 
clearly indicates that Shri Wasik was working as a Contractor 
and a few persons were employed by him as contract laboll 
ICTS . Shri Mansingh WAS One of them till he was stopped 
from work with effect from 25- 1 - 1973 when on his own 
admission he absented himself from duty . The failure report 
a9 well as pleadings go to show that the dispute was about 
illegal stoppage of work from 25 - 1 - 1975 but the reference 
speaks of the illegal stoppage of work with ellect from 
2 - 3 - 1975 . In fact as discussed above there was 10 stoppage 
with effect from 2 - 3 - 1975 as Shri Mansingh was not in em 
ployment on that date . Tho reference is thus materially 
different from the dispute that was raised by the Union . 


10 . For all these reasons it is obvious that there was no 
relationship of employer and employee between the Com 
pany and Shri Mansingh . As Shri Mansingh was not a 
workman of the Company no industrial dispute could be 
agitated with respect to him and on that account no indus 
trial dispute came into existence , and as the releicncc rclates 
to a date materially different from the date of the dispute 
as it was originally raised , it is held that the reference is 
invalid in law . Award is given accordogly . 


6 . Shri S . L . Sachdeva ( M . W . 2 ) who was the Manager of 
Newton Chickli B Colliery at that time has also stated on 
oath that Shri Mansingh was an employee of the Contractor, 
There is Another memo addressed to Shri T , K . Banerjee 
signed by the Manager of the Colliery arking Shri Banerico 
" to allow Shri Mansingh Contractor worker on duty from 
tomorrow , the 19th February 1975 . ” Shri Mansingh has 
admitted that he went to Shri Banerjee with that letter who 
wrote back on Ex . W / 1 that Wasik Contractor does not 
like to take him on his roll. I have no objection in allowing 
him on duty , This document clearly goes to show that Shri 
Manşingh was an employee of the contractor and his em 
ployer i. e . Shri Wasik Contractor did not want to take him 
back on the roll. The management of the Company had no 
objection to his coming back on duty if the employer of 
Shri Mansingh was agreeable to take him back . 

7 . Shri S . L . Sachdeva ( M . W . 2 ) stated that Shri Wasik 
Contractor was given the work of stone cutting, debris 
cleaning, etc . He used to employ labour independently and 
thoso labourers were under his control and paid by him , 
The Manager used to ask the Contractor to do a 
details of which were left to the contractor as to how many 
labourers were to he employed for the execution of that 
assignment. Shri Sachdeva , Manager , has again said speci 
fically that he had no authority to transfer any labourer 
of the contractor from one place to the other, Quarterly 
bonus, Profit Sharing bonus. Leave etc and Leave Travel 
Concession were being paid to the Contractor s labour directly 
by the management debitable to the account of the Contractor . 
This was being done in terms of the agreement between the 
Contractor and the management with a vicw to secure the 
wages and other benefits to the omployees of the Contractor. 
Such payments , therefore , were not sufficient to make them 
Assume the status of tho workmen of the Company and thus 
they could not create A direct relationship of employer and 
employee, master and scivant, between Shri Mansingh 
and C . M . A . 


S . N . JOHRI, Presiding Officer

2 . Management s case is that tho lady was appointed tem

___ This is a complaint under section 33 - A of the Industrial 
Disputes Act challenging the validity of the order No. 
EL / ( P ) / BSP / D & A / SRM / 85 dated 13 - 7 - 1977 passed by the 
Divisional Electrical Engineer changing servicc conditions of 
the complainant during the pendency of his Labour Couit 
application No. 108 of 1977 . 


2 . The complainant was charge -sheeted on 17 - 6 - 1976 for 
the delinquency committed on 8 - 10-1974 ind n minor nenalty 
WAS inflicted after considering his reply but no orders were 
passed al that time about the emoluments payable to him for

the period from 8 - 10 - 1974 to 1 - 5- 1975 during which he had

which claim was registered as Labour Court Case No . 108

the New India Assurance Co . Ltd ., Bombay a sub

3 . During the pendency of that application the competent 
authority passed in order that the suspension period shull 
be treated as Suspension and nothing more than the suspension 
allowance alrcudy paid to him shall be admissible . It is 
alleged that this order changed the service conditions of the 
workman during the pendency of Labour Court application 
before this Couit and that too without accking prior pci 
inission . 


2 . In order to appreciate the preliminary objection raised 
to the milintainability of the Reference on account of juriy 
diction of this Tribunal, it is necessary to set out in short 
the facts of the case . 


4 . As per Rule 2044 and 2044 - B of Ruifway Establish 
ment Code, Volume 1l the Management was competent to 
pass order s with respect to the wages of Suspension period 
when the domestic cnquiry resulted in the award of punish 
inent to the delinquent cmployee cven though the punishment 
was only a minor penalty . Management thus only corrected 
thc ster which it had omitted to take. An action so tohen 
under the Rules, even during the pendency of procecdings 
before this Court was only a bonafide act and will not amount 
to alternation of service conditions within the meaning of 
Section 33 ( 1 ) of the Industiil Disputes Act. It has been 
so held repeatedly by the labour Appellate Tribunal and 
cven by the Supreme Court. There was no brcach of the 
Provisions of Section 33 ( 1 ) of the Act and the order so pass 
ed did not require prior permission of this Court for it s 
validity . 


3. Consequent upon the nationalisation of General Insu 
lance business with effect from 1 - 1 - 1973 , 20 employees in 
the Insurancc Department of the Kciala State Government 
were rendered surplus and were transferred to the General 
Insurance Corporation of indla . At the timc of the transfer 
of these employees , the quication of revision of pay and 
illowances of the Government servants in Kerala were 
pending consideration of the State Pay Commission . These 
20 employees who were involved in this case weię ushed 
to join the services of the Corporation with effect from 
1 - 9 - 1973. By their Oiclei duted 5 - 4 - 1974 the Goveroment 
of Kerala revised the scales of pay of their employces and 
Lave pay levision with retrospective effect from 1 - 7 - 1973 . 
These 20 employees were paid the Aftears as per the revised 
scales for the period from 1 - 7 - 1973 to 31- 8 - 1973. By the 
Notification dated 27 - 5 - 1974 , the Government of India 
framed the General Insurance (Rationalisation and Revision 
of Pay Scales and other conditions of Services of Super 
visory , Clcrical and Subordinate Staff ) Scheme, 1974 , 
Pura 6 of this Scheme deals with the method of fixation 
in the new scales. So far as these 20 cmployees arc con 
cerned , they were fixed in the revised scales as provided 
in para 6 ( a ) of the Scheme. Contention on behalf of these 
20 employces is that they should have been fixed in terms of 
para 6 ( C ) of the Scheme. 


5. The complaint is , thereforc , not maintainable and is 
hereby dismissed . Award in glven accordingly ,

AWARD 
1. The Central Government his leferred the following 
dispute for adjudication by this Tribunal : 

SCHEDULE 


5 . Section 16 ( 1 ) ( 8 ) of the General Insurance Business 
(Nationalisation ) Act, 1972 ( hereinafter referred to as the 
Act of 1972 ) provides 11 Scheme for reorganisation of General 
Insurance Business . Amongst other things the scheme is to 
provide for the nationalisation or revision of pay scales 
ind other terms and conditions of service of officers and 
other cmployees wherever nccessary . In exercise of powers 
conferred by Section 16 ( 1 ) ( ) of the Act of 1972 such 1 
Scheme has been prescribed and it may be referred to as 
Scheme of 1974 , Sub - section 7 of Section 16 of the Act 
of 1972 lays down that " the provisions of this section and 
of any scheme framed under it shall have effect notwith 
standing anything to the contrary contained in any other law 
or any agreement, award or other instrument for the time 
being in force . " It has been veheniently argued for the 
Company that the effect of this sub - section is that the Scheme 
in a self - contained code and for the icsolution of any doubt 
or dificulty as to the interpretation of any provision of 
this Schene, recourse should be had to the provision of 
the Scheme and not to any outside agency . 

6 . Paragraph 21 of the Schemc provides a machinery (18 
to the interpretation of the terms of this Scheme. It says 
that where any doubt or difficulty arises as to the interpreta

sidering the provisions of Section 36 ( 1 ) of the Act, Shri

" On the facts of this case it is clear that the reference
